## ■ 2.3 Reasoning robustness under information contamination

B<sub>eyon</sub>d <sub>or</sub>di<sub>nary errors,</sub> LLM <sub>reason</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>s vu</sub>l<sub>nera</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o con</sub>t<sub>am</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>npu</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> Mi<sub>s</sub>i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s one</sub> f<sub>orm o</sub>f <sub>a</sub> b<sub>roa</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>rea</sub>t<sub>:</sub> th<sub>e man</sub>i<sub>pu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> of the reasoning process itself—of which injected false premises, <sub>ersuas</sub>i<sub>ve ressure an</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>am</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence are a</sub>ll i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ances.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> f<sub>rame</sub> th<sub>e ro</sub>bl<sub>em a</sub>t thi<sub>s</sub> l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>o</sub>f <sub>enera</sub>lit <sub>an</sub>d <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e on</sub> it<sub>s</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t<sub>-</sub> d<sub>ocumen</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ance.</sub> P<sub>r</sub>i<sub>or wor</sub>k <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ows</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>m</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on propaga</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>a</sub>l<sub>ong reason</sub>i<sub>ng c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns: even w</sub>h<sub>en mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s possess correc</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>l knowledge, injected false premises reduce knowledgeable accuracy b 10–72% [6], and ex licit correction instructions succeed in fewer than half of cases. Reasoning post-training (thinking models) does <sub>no</sub>t <sub>era</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e vu</sub>l<sub>nera</sub>bilit<sub>y, w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>race</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub> t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng s</sub>t<sub>age ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> t<sub>o reason</sub>i<sub>ng capac</sub>it<sub>y</sub> it<sub>se</sub>lf<sub>;</sub> th<sub>e propose</sub>d <sub>reme</sub>d<sub>y—</sub>fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng on ear</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>correc</sub>ti<sub>on—</sub>i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng-</sub>ti<sub>me an</sub>d model-bound [6]. The same mechanism anal sis shows that correction is most efective when applied early in the reasoning trajectory, before the contaminated <sub>p</sub>remise <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>a<sub>g</sub>ates downstream [6]; this is <sub>p</sub>recisel<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng our</sub> t<sub>wo-s</sub>t<sub>age</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gn en</sub>f<sub>orces a</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> ti<sub>me—</sub>th<sub>e</sub> verification sta<sub>g</sub>e (candidate × evidence matrix) alwa<sub>y</sub>s <sub>p</sub>recedes the causal-anal<sub>y</sub>sis sta<sub>g</sub>e (Section 3), so <sub>p</sub>remises are checked before an<sub>y</sub> d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>va</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>u</sub>ild<sub>s on</sub> th<sub>em.</sub>

Th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>a</sub>il<sub>ure a</sub>tt<sub>ern</sub> i<sub>s no</sub>t <sub>mac</sub>hi<sub>ne-s ec</sub>ifi<sub>c:</sub> i<sub>n</sub> h<sub>uman reason</sub>i<sub>n , m</sub>i<sub>s-</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on con</sub>ti<sub>nues</sub> t<sub>o s</sub>h<sub>ape</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence even a</sub>ft<sub>er rec</sub>i<sub>p</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t<sub>s exp</sub>li<sub>c</sub>itl<sub>y</sub> accept a correction—the continued influence efect [23, 24], traced to memor<sub>y</sub> failure [25]. On the machine side, reasonin<sub>g</sub>-o<sub>p</sub>timized models resist majorit ressure in social-influence simulations (conformit 3.13% vs. u<sub>p</sub> to 19.45%) [26], and enablin<sub>g</sub> thinkin<sub>g</sub> mode reduces <sub>p</sub>ersuasion rates in multi-a<sub>g</sub>ent <sub>p</sub>ersuasion ex<sub>p</sub>eriments [27]. O<sub>ur</sub> f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>s</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l i<sub>s an ex</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>ll<sub>y en</sub>f<sub>orce</sub>d<sub>, ev</sub>id<sub>ence-</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>ki<sub>ng s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ure o</sub>f thi<sub>s</sub> ki<sub>n</sub>d<sub>: a can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e surv</sub>i<sub>ves on</sub>l <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en suppor</sub>t<sub>-</sub> i<sub>ng ev</sub>id<sub>ence ou</sub>t<sub>num</sub>b<sub>ers oppos</sub>i<sub>ng ev</sub>id<sub>ence, an</sub>d th<sub>e re-exam</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>oo re-o ens</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on w</sub>h<sub>enever</sub> f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>s</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>s s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t<sub>.</sub> A<sub>s an</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence-</sub>ti<sub>me,</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng-</sub>f<sub>ree</sub> l<sub>ayer</sub> it <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng-</sub>ti<sub>me correc-</sub> ti<sub>ons an</sub>d t<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>ers across mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s; an</sub>d b<sub>ecause res</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ance comes</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> evidence checking rather than stance retention, it yields correctable insistence, avoidin the error-entrenchment failure mode of stancebased defenses [26 27]. ## ■ 3.1 Overview: a five-stage causal-chain closed loop

Th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ramewor</sub>k f<sub>orma</sub>li<sub>zes</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on as a c</sub>l<sub>ose</sub>d l<sub>oop o</sub>f fi<sub>ve s</sub>t<sub>ages:</sub>

The model is <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d t<sub>o reason a</sub>l<sub>ong a</sub> f<sub>orce</sub>d <sub>c</sub>h<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n—</sub>f<sub>our-exam</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>on ana</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>s</sub> → ei<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>p</sub>rinci<sub>p</sub>les → disease nature → disease location → s<sub>y</sub>ndrome synthesis—explicitly forbidden to jump from a single sign to a s<sub>y</sub>n<sup>d</sup>rome.

■ 3.2 Candidate × evidence matrix falsification protocol Th<sub>e pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e core o</sub>f thi<sub>s wor</sub>k<sub>, cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l’<sub>s conc</sub>l<sub>us</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>process as</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ows:</sub>

• Matrix construction. Rows are candidate syndromes; columns are th<sub>e pa</sub>ti<sub>en</sub>t’<sub>s</sub> f<sub>our-exam</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>on ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> it<sub>ems.</sub>

• Cell judgment. Each cell is judged support, oppose, or irrelevant, justified by the injected diagnostic standards.

• Row aggregation. A candidate is compatible ifsupporting evidence <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>num</sub>b<sub>ers</sub> <sub>oppos</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> <sub>an</sub>d k<sub>ey</sub> <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>suppor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>;</sub> <sub>o</sub>th<sub>erw</sub>i<sub>se</sub> it is incompatible.

• Suficiency rule (falsification). The number of incompatible candidates must be at least half of the candidate space; otherwise dif<sub>eren</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>eeme</sub>d i<sub>nsu</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>en</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence mus</sub>t b<sub>e re-exam</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

• Selection and output. The final conclusion is the com atible candidate with the most su<sub>pp</sub>ortin<sub>g</sub> evidence (or several, in multianswer settin<sub>g</sub>s), emitted on a sin<sub>g</sub>le line in a fixed marker format ([Final Syndrome]: X)—mac<sup>hi</sup>ne-extracta<sup>bl</sup>e an<sup>d</sup> c<sup>h</sup>ec<sup>k</sup>a<sup>bl</sup>e.

• Prohibitions. Choosing by candidate order and copying example <sub>conc</sub>l<sub>us</sub>i<sub>ons are ex</sub> li<sub>c</sub>itl b<sub>anne</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

• Explicit refutation. An opposed cell judgment must name the contradictin<sub>g</sub> evidence (e.<sub>g</sub>., “o<sub>pp</sub>osed b<sub>y</sub> E3”). Silent elimination <sub>o</sub>f <sub>a can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> di<sub>scourage</sub>d<sub>: memory-up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ng researc</sub>h <sub>s</sub>h<sub>ows</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>correc</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>exp</sub>li<sub>c</sub>itl<sub>y</sub> <sub>re-s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> di<sub>scre</sub>dit<sub>e</sub>d it<sub>em—co-ac</sub>ti<sub>va</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> it <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e con</sub>fli<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ng ev</sub>id<sub>ence—up</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> b<sub>e</sub>li<sub>e</sub>f<sub>s more e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>an</sub> corrections that onl<sub>y</sub> su<sub>pp</sub>l<sub>y</sub> the correct answer [45], and the lo<sub>gg</sub>ed <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er ma</sub>k<sub>es eac</sub>h f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>s</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on au</sub>dit<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e.</sub>

Every cell judgment can be cross-checked against the evidence <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>kli<sub>s</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d di<sub>a nos</sub>ti<sub>c s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d<sub>s, ma</sub>ki<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e reason</sub>i<sub>n w</sub>hit<sub>e-</sub>b<sub>ox</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>au</sub>dit<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e.</sub> Fi<sub>gure</sub> 3 ill<sub>us</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l <sub>on a wor</sub>k<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>ree-</sub> <sub>can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e examp</sub>l<sub>e.</sub> F<sub>orma</sub>l <sub>rea</sub>di<sub>ngs o</sub>f th<sub>e pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l <sub>are co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> G<sub>:</sub> a belief-revision (AGM) readin<sub>g</sub> of the a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ation, contraction, and <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency ru</sub>l<sub>es;</sub> th<sub>e proo</sub>f <sub>o</sub>f P<sub>ropos</sub>iti<sub>on</sub> 1 <sub>w</sub>ith it<sub>s cavea</sub>t<sub>s.</sub>

## • Statistical reading: sequential multi-hypothesis testing

T<sub>rea</sub>t th<sub>e can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e se</sub>t $\mathcal { H } = \{ h _ { 1 } , \ldots , h _ { m } \}$ <sup>as</sup> � <sup>com</sup>p<sup>etin</sup>g <sup>h</sup>yp<sup>othe-</sup> <sub>ses an</sub>d <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> it<sub>em</sub> $e _ { j }$ <sub>as an o</sub>b<sub>serva</sub>ti<sub>on;</sub> th<sub>e ce</sub>ll f<sub>unc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> $f ( h _ { i } , e _ { j } ) ~ \in ~ \{ + 1 , 0 , - 1 \}$ i<sub>s a per-</sub>h<sub>ypo</sub>th<sub>es</sub>i<sub>s score.</sub> D<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> th<sub>e row</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>c</sub>

$$

Declarative skill files. Textbook standards (chiefly TCM Diagnostics [49]) and the above datasets are refined into 23 Markdown skill files or<sub>g</sub>anized into six s<sub>p</sub>ecialt<sub>y</sub> skills (basic dia<sub>g</sub>nosis, insomnia, h<sub>yp</sub>ertension, <sub>gy</sub>necolo<sub>gy</sub>, <sub>p</sub>ediatrics, acu<sub>p</sub>uncture). Skills are routed by specialty priority and injected per lifecycle stage, so <sub>p</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>c</sub>i<sub>ans can rev</sub>i<sub>se</sub> k<sub>now</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t t<sub>ouc</sub>hi<sub>ng co</sub>d<sub>e.</sub>

At diferentiation time, two complementary anchors are injected alongside the forced causal chain. Theory anchor (methodological rules). Key decision rules distilled from textbooks, e.g., a thick coating alone cannot exclude deficiency syndromes; the absence of a greasy coating alone cannot exclude phlegm-heat; a pale-red tongue alone cannot exclude liver-fire or phlegm-heat; a pale-purple tongue alone does not diagnose blood stasis; and a symptom-priority principle when signs conflict. Data anchor (statistical clues). $$
\begin{array} { r l r } & { \scriptstyle \Xi ( \mathrm { P R E S C R I B E } \implies \bigodot \big ( \mathrm { R E V I E W ~ V ~ A B O R T E D } \big ) ) , } & { \scriptstyle ( 6 ) } \\ & { \scriptstyle \Xi ( \mathrm { ( c o L L E C T ~ \wedge ~ c o m p l e t e n e s s ~ < 0 . 6 ~ \wedge ~ \  f o r c e d ) ~ \Rightarrow ~ \bigodot ~ C o L L E C T } ) , } & \end{array}\tag{7}
$$

where □ is “ loball ”, ⃝ “next”, ^ “eventuall ”, and forced marks the forced-advance <sub>p</sub>aths of Section 3.4 (turn-count fallback, deadlock truncation, timeout <sub>g</sub>uards). The first invariant requires ever<sub>y</sub> prescribe t<sub>o</sub> b<sub>e even</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>ll f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>owe</sub>d b <sub>a review or an a</sub>b<sub>or</sub>t<sub>, a</sub>li<sub>gne</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> PLAN→PRESCRIBE→REVIEW order of Table 1; the second is the COLLECT-exit precondition restricted to non-forced trajectories (sto<sub>p</sub>-si<sub>g</sub>nal advancement needs no exclusion because it first raises com<sub>p</sub>leteness to the threshold). Both are enforced at runtime b<sub>y</sub> construction and are checkable post hoc on any logged trajectory, which is what makes the audit la<sub>y</sub>er (Section 5.8) a verification ste<sub>p</sub> rather th<sub>an a</sub> l<sub>og</sub> d<sub>ump.</sub> B<sub>ecause</sub> th<sub>e au</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>t<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> fi<sub>n</sub>it<sub>e an</sub>d th<sub>e guar</sub>d<sub>s are</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>id<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e pre</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>es,</sub> th<sub>ese proper</sub>ti<sub>es are</sub> i<sub>n pr</sub>i<sub>nc</sub>i<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>-c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>k<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>over</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub>’<sub>s</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>iti<sub>on grap</sub>h<sub>; we</sub> l<sub>eave mec</sub>h<sub>an</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>c</sub>h<sub>ec</sub>ki<sub>n</sub> t<sub>o</sub> f<sub>u</sub>t<sub>ure wor</sub>k<sub>.</sub>

Table 1 Mapping between method stages and lifecycle states.
<table><tr><td>Method stage</td><td>Lifecycle state(s)</td></tr><tr><td>Intent triage, direct retrieval</td><td>outside the machine</td></tr><tr><td>Information collection, sufficiency</td><td>COLLECT</td></tr><tr><td>Hypothesis &amp; evidence construction</td><td>DIAGNOSE (prompt injection)</td></tr><tr><td>Matrix falsification &amp; output</td><td>DIAGNOSE (output protocol)</td></tr><tr><td>Causal-chain reasoning</td><td>DIAGNOSE (reasoning)</td></tr><tr><td>Action: plan, prescribe, review</td><td>PLAN → PRESCRIBE → REVIEW</td></tr><tr><td>Feedback &amp; consistency</td><td>FOLLOWUP → COMPLETED</td></tr><tr><td>Runtime protection &amp; audit</td><td>cross-cutting</td></tr><tr><td>Hard violations, fatal signals</td><td>ABORTED</td></tr></table>

Feedback and recovery. Runtime signals (tool failures, safet violations, etc.) are <sub>g</sub>raded into four severities—info, warnin<sub>g</sub>, severe, fatal—mapped to six responses (ignore, warn, adjust, pause, roll back, abort). Hard-constraint si<sub>g</sub>nals are handled s<sub>y</sub>nchronousl<sub>y</sub> (tar<sub>g</sub>et latenc 50 ms), and fatal si<sub>g</sub>nals drive the state machine into ABORTED. Sna<sub>p</sub>shots are saved automaticall<sub>y</sub> on ever<sub>y</sub> state chan<sub>g</sub>e (SHA-256 inte<sub>g</sub>rit<sub>y</sub>, last 10 versions retained); failures follow a three-level <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub>— <sub>au</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>ti<sub>c re</sub>t<sub>ry w</sub>ithi<sub>n</sub> 5 <sub>s, ro</sub>llb<sub>ac</sub>k t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub>t <sub>sa</sub>f<sub>e snaps</sub>h<sub>o</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n</sub> 30 <sub>s, or manua</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erven</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Runtime robustness. Four cross-cutting subsystems keep long <sub>consu</sub>lt<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e on sma</sub>ll <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s:</sub> l<sub>a ere</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t b<sub>u</sub>d <sub>e</sub>ti<sub>n across</sub> <sub>p</sub>ermanent/session/task windows; streamin<sub>g</sub> <sub>g</sub>uardrails (hard token ca<sub>p</sub>s, re<sub>p</sub>etition-loo<sub>p</sub> and lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e-mixin<sub>g</sub> detection); five-dimension health monitoring; and trajectory logging with privacy de-identification. C d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>a</sub>il<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on ru</sub>l<sub>es.</sub>

## ■ 3.8 Model-agnostic adapter and deployment

A unified ada<sub>p</sub>ter (local Transformers / vLLM cluster / O<sub>p</sub>enAIcom<sub>p</sub>atible APIs / mock) decou<sub>p</sub>les the mechanism from an<sub>y</sub> s<sub>p</sub>ecific <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>, w</sub>ith <sub>au</sub>t<sub>oma</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> d<sub>ev</sub>i<sub>ce</sub> d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> thi<sub>n</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub>/<sub>reason</sub>i<sub>ng separa</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> logprob confidence, and parameter auto-scaling of knowledge-injection b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s across mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>zes.</sub>

## ■ 4 Experiments

## ■ 4.1 Dataset

We evaluate on TCMEval-SDT [39] (CC-BY 4.0), the public bench-<sub>mar</sub>k f<sub>or</sub> TCM <sub>syn</sub>d<sub>rome-</sub>dif<sub>eren</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on reason</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub> It <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> 300 <sub>exper</sub>t<sub>-anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>cases;</sub> <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> 200<sub>-case</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>sp</sub>lit i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>es</sub> <sub>go</sub>ld answers (multi-select amon<sub>g</sub> 10 o<sub>p</sub>tions A–J for both <sub>p</sub>atho<sub>g</sub>enesis and s<sub>y</sub>ndrome), and is therefore used for evaluation. Gold s<sub>y</sub>ndrome sets <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> 1<sub>–</sub>4 it<sub>ems an</sub>d <sub>pa</sub>th<sub>ogenes</sub>i<sub>s se</sub>t<sub>s</sub> 1<sub>–</sub>6 it<sub>ems.</sub>

## ■ 4.2 Evaluation protocol

Options are injected as single-quoted candidate markers into the agent i<sub>npu</sub>t<sub>,</sub> t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>gger</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l<sub>;</sub> t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>conc</sub>l<sub>us</sub>i<sub>ons are ex</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d and mapped back to option letters. ## ■ 4.3 Evaluation hygiene

Three declarations address evaluation-contamination concerns. (i) Knowledge–benchmark overlap. (ii) Knowledge-iteration history. ## ■ 4.5 Configurations

• Qwen3-4B / Qwen3-32B [52]: zero-shot direct answering, and with the full framework (local vLLM);

• PT-4B + agent: a TCM post-trained 4B model with the full f<sub>ramewor</sub>k<sub>;</sub>

• DeepSeek-V4-Flash [53]: zero-shot and with the framework (oficial API);

• DeepSeek-V4-Pro: zero-shot and with the framework (oficial API);

• K3 zero-shot / K3 + agent: Kimi K3 (2026 release) [54] without <sub>an</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ramewor</sub>k<sub>.</sub>

Pa er baselines (Gemini 1.5 Pro ≈0.475 s ndrome, ChatGLM-130B ≈0.532 <sub>p</sub>atho<sub>g</sub>enesis, converted from the <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er’s wei<sub>g</sub>hted sums to <sub>p</sub>er-case means) are shown for reference; note the<sub>y</sub> are 2023–2024-era <sub>zero-s</sub>h<sub>o</sub>t <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>ons on a ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub> 50<sub>-case su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

## ■ 4.6 Ablation design

Startin<sub>g</sub> from the full a<sub>g</sub>ent (G0, Qwen3-4B), we remove one component per roup: G1 no matrix-and-format instruction block (the f<sub>a</sub>l<sub>s</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on-ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x requ</sub>i<sub>remen</sub>t<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e-</sub>b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> <sub>em</sub>i<sub>ss</sub>i<sub>on man</sub>d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e, an</sub>d th<sub>e assoc</sub>i<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pro</sub>hibiti<sub>ons are one promp</sub>t block and are removed jointly; knowledge injection is retained); G2 no evidence checklist/authoritative standards; G3 no diferential-diagnosis guide (insomnia-tuned); G4 no skill-knowledge injection at all. Each <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub> is re-evaluated on both tasks (200 cases each, three re<sub>p</sub>etitions). A separateformat-only baseline—candidate markers and the mandatory single-line output format, but no matrix and no knowledge injection— separates the format layer from the content layer that G1 removes jointly, and is run on both the bud et-matched Qwen3-4B and Dee Seek-V4- Flash. On Qwen3-4B the factorial is completed b two further arms: a no-matrix arm (G5, content + format, no matrix requirement) and a no-format arm (G6, content + matrix, no emission mandate).

## ■ 5.2 Mechanistic attribution: format–content complementarity

Which la er carries these efects? The G1 ablation (Table 8) removes the matrix requirement and the output-format mandate jointly (both live in a sin<sub>g</sub>le instruction block in the de<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub>ed <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t), so it cannot se<sub>p</sub>arate them. (i) The format/candidate-bounding layer is sign-conditional: it resc es the tr ncation- rone model (flash s ndrome +0 124 ans erextraction rate 38.5%→75.0% s<sub>y</sub>ndrome, 33.0%→49.5% <sub>p</sub>atho<sub>g</sub>enesis) but costs the disci<sub>p</sub>lined one (4B), su<sub>pp</sub>ressin<sub>g</sub> free causal reasonin<sub>g</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o can</sub>did<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e osc</sub>ill<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> 4B <sub>ne a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>dli<sub>ne o</sub>f S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 5<sub>.</sub>1 i<sub>s</sub> attributable rimaril to this la er. (ii) The causal chain and the format layer are complements, not additive layers: on 4B the causal chain pays of only jointly with the format, and the sign flips across the two <sub>con</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>ons;</sub> th<sub>e ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x an</sub>d k<sub>now</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge marg</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l<sub>s are separa</sub>t<sub>e</sub>l<sub>y</sub> nil (see (iii)). (iii) The matrix’s own marginal contribution to score is nil-to-negative on this model. Two independent estimates agree: with format resent full − (content+format no matrix) = −0.027/−0.008 ( =0.12/0.57, n.s.); without format, (content+matrix, no format) − (content onl ) = −0.054/−0.036 ( =0.018/0.17). ## ■ 6.1 The protocol sets the floor, capability the ceiling

## ■ 6.2 Why two years of model progress barely moves this benchmark

A <sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>iki<sub>ng</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t i<sub>n</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 2 i<sub>s</sub> h<sub>ow</sub> littl<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>e</sub>ld’<sub>s</sub> t<sub>wo-year progress</sub> achieves on this benchmark: from Gemini 1.5 Pro (2024, ≈0.475 s<sub>y</sub>ndrome) to Dee<sub>p</sub>Seek-V4-Pro, K3, and Qwen3-32B (2026, 0.443– 0<sub>.</sub>551 <sub>on s n</sub>d<sub>rome—</sub>b<sub>rac</sub>k<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> 2024 f<sub>ron</sub>ti<sub>er ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an excee</sub>di<sub>n</sub> it), zero-shot scores move onl a few <sub>p</sub>oints, while <sub>g</sub>eneral ca<sub>p</sub>abilit b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s ga</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d t<sub>wen</sub>t<sub>y po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s or more over</sub> th<sub>e same per</sub>i<sub>o</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>cross-era compar</sub>i<sub>son</sub> i<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>ve ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>n</sub>iti<sub>ve—</sub>th<sub>e</sub> 2023<sub>–</sub> 2024 b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes are zero-s</sub>h<sub>o</sub>t <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>ons on a ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub> 50<sub>-case su</sub>b<sub>se</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>conver</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom we</sub>i ht<sub>e</sub>d <sub>sums—</sub>b<sub>u</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>across</sub> <sub>a</sub>ll fi<sub>ve o</sub>f <sub>our own</sub> 2026<sub>-era zero-s</sub>h<sub>o</sub>t <sub>runs.</sub> W<sub>e see</sub> f<sub>our compoun</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> causes, and one consequence. First, the task tests a thought process, not knowledge: contrastive discrimination amon similar s ndromes is s arsel re resented in trainin cor ora (case literature records conclusions, rarel<sub>y</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>licit elimination), so <sub>g</sub>eneral ca<sub>p</sub>abilit<sub>y g</sub>ains transfer weakl . Second, the metric punishes deliberation: the oficial <sub>scr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>t <sub>score</sub> di<sub>v</sub>id<sub>es</sub> b<sub>y</sub> $\vert A \vert + \vert B \setminus A \vert .$ <sub>, so any over-se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s c</sub>h<sub>arge</sub>d directl ; stron er models tend to select more (our failure anal sis finds over- rediction in about 30% of cases) which ca s free-form generation near 0.5–0.55. Third, the field’s main direction of progress is misaligned with the task: lon chain-of-thou ht evolution rewards l<sub>onger</sub> d<sub>e</sub>lib<sub>era</sub>ti<sub>on, w</sub>h<sub>ereas</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>rewar</sub>d<sub>s convergen</sub>t <sub>compar</sub>i<sub>son—</sub> the model with the lon<sub>g</sub>est reasonin<sub>g</sub> chains in our cam<sub>p</sub>ai<sub>g</sub>n (DS-Flash, routinel<sub>y</sub> >10k reasonin<sub>g</sub> tokens) scored worst zero-shot (0.289) and <sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d th<sub>e mos</sub>t <sub>em</sub> t <sub>ou</sub>t <sub>u</sub>t<sub>s, w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e conc</sub>i<sub>se-reason</sub>i<sub>n</sub> DS<sub>-</sub>P<sub>ro</sub> (≈1k reasoning tokens to termination) scored best. ## • Reproducibility

<sup>Th</sup>e ana<sup>l</sup>ys<sup>i</sup>s scr<sup>i</sup>pts (L1 \*.py) an<sup>d</sup> mac<sup>hi</sup>ne-rea<sup>d</sup>a<sup>bl</sup>e outputs are <sub>arc</sub>hi<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d <sub>re</sub>l<sub>ease</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on co</sub>d<sub>e.</sub>

## ■ G Belief-revision reading of the matrix protocol

Th<sub>e ro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l’<sub>s com onen</sub>t<sub>s can</sub> b<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e voca</sub>b<sub>u</sub>l<sub>ar o</sub>f b<sub>e</sub>li<sub>e</sub>f<sub>-</sub> revision theor<sub>y</sub> [63,64], <sub>g</sub>ivin<sub>g</sub> the en<sub>g</sub>ineerin<sub>g</sub> choices a lo<sub>g</sub>ical anchor. The formal objects are the enumerated candidate set $H = \left\{ h _ { 1 } , \dots , h _ { m } \right\}$ <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> it<sub>ems</sub> $E = \{ e _ { 1 } , \ldots , e _ { n } \}$ with their cell judgments:

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { \nu ( h , e ) \in \{ + 1 ( \mathrm { s u p p o r t } ) , - 1 ( \mathrm { o p p o s e } ) , 0 ( \mathrm { i r r e l e v a n t } ) \} , } \\ & { \quad s ( h ) = \# \{ e : \nu ( h , e ) = + 1 \} , } \\ & { \quad o ( h ) = \# \{ e : \nu ( h , e ) = - 1 \} . } \end{array}\tag{9}
$$

Three corres ondences hold. $$

Two connections tie this reading to the rest of the framework. (i) The <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x score</sub> $\begin{array} { r } { S ( h _ { i } ) = \sum _ { j } w _ { j } f ( h _ { i } , e _ { j } ) } \end{array}$ of Section 3.2 is a ternary quantization of exactly this additive log-posterior update: $f \in \{ + 1 , 0 , - 1 \}$ th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld<sub>s eac</sub>h <sub>per-</sub>it<sub>em</sub> l<sub>og-pos</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>or</sub> i<sub>ncremen</sub>t<sub>,</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>di<sub>ng granu</sub>l<sub>ar</sub>it<sub>y</sub> f<sub>or ce</sub>ll<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>au</sub>dit<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y an</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-error ana</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>s o</sub>f P<sub>ropo-</sub> <sub>s</sub>iti<sub>on</sub> 1<sub>—w</sub>h<sub>ose con</sub>diti<sub>ona</sub>l<sub>-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>ence assump</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e same</sub> assumption under which the pooling of Eq. (10) is exact. (ii) The reading clarifies what prompt-level injection ofers over weight-level i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on: pr</sub>i<sub>or</sub> f<sub>ragmen</sub>t<sub>s, cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s, an</sub>d <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>s a</sub>ll <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub> inspectable, physician-revisable data while the base model performs th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>egra</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> W<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e scope exp</sub>li<sub>c</sub>itl<sub>y:</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ramewor</sub>k d<sub>oes</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>m</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>compu</sub>t<sub>es a ca</sub>lib<sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>pos</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>or, on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t the injected uantities decom ose the decision into auditable rior, likelihood, and constraint terms whose <sub>p</sub>rovenance is lo<sub>gg</sub>ed [65].
